   300	// Compare compares the two label sets.
   301	// The result will be 0 if a==b, <0 if a < b, and >0 if a > b.
   302	func Compare(a, b Labels) int {
   303		l := min(len(b), len(a))
   304	
   305		for i := range l {
   306			if a[i].Name != b[i].Name {
   307				if a[i].Name < b[i].Name {
   308					return -1
   309				}
   310				return 1
   311			}
   312			if a[i].Value != b[i].Value {
   313				if a[i].Value < b[i].Value {
   314					return -1
   315				}
   316				return 1
   317			}
   318		}
   319		// If all labels so far were in common, the set with fewer labels comes first.
   320		return len(a) - len(b)
   321	}
   322

   333	// Compare compares the two label sets.
   334	// The result will be 0 if a==b, <0 if a < b, and >0 if a > b.
   335	func Compare(a, b Labels) int {
   336		// Find the first byte in the string where a and b differ.
   337		shorter, longer := a.data, b.data
   338		if len(b.data) < len(a.data) {
   339			shorter, longer = b.data, a.data
   340		}
   341		i := 0
   342		// First, go 8 bytes at a time. Data strings are expected to be 8-byte aligned.
   343		sp := unsafe.Pointer(unsafe.StringData(shorter))
   344		lp := unsafe.Pointer(unsafe.StringData(longer))
   345		for ; i < len(shorter)-8; i += 8 {
   346			if *(*uint64)(unsafe.Add(sp, i)) != *(*uint64)(unsafe.Add(lp, i)) {
   347				break
   348			}
   349		}
   350		// Now go 1 byte at a time.
   351		for ; i < len(shorter); i++ {
   352			if shorter[i] != longer[i] {
   353				break
   354			}
   355		}
   356		if i == len(shorter) {
   357			// One Labels was a prefix of the other; the set with fewer labels compares lower.
   358			return len(a.data) - len(b.data)
   359		}
   360

   483	// Compare compares the two label sets.
   484	// The result will be 0 if a==b, <0 if a < b, and >0 if a > b.
   485	func Compare(a, b Labels) int {
   486		la, lb := len(a.data), len(b.data)
   487		ia, ib := 0, 0
   488		for ia < la && ib < lb {
   489			var aName, bName string
   490			aName, ia = decodeString(a.syms, a.data, ia)
   491			bName, ib = decodeString(b.syms, b.data, ib)
   492			if aName != bName {
   493				if aName < bName {
   494					return -1
   495				}
   496				return 1
   497			}
   498			var aValue, bValue string
   499			aValue, ia = decodeString(a.syms, a.data, ia)
   500			bValue, ib = decodeString(b.syms, b.data, ib)
   501			if aValue != bValue {
   502				if aValue < bValue {
   503					return -1
   504				}
   505				return 1
   506			}
   507		}
   508		// If all labels so far were in common, the set with fewer labels comes first.
   509		return (la - ia) - (lb - ib)
   510	}
   511

   810	
   811	`sort_by_label(v instant-vector, label string, ...)` returns vector elements
   812	sorted by the values of the given labels in ascending order. In case these
   813	label values are equal, elements are sorted by their full label sets.
   814	`sort_by_label` acts on float and histogram samples in the same way.
   815	
   816	Please note that `sort_by_label` only affects the results of instant queries, as
   817	range query results always have a fixed output ordering.
   818	
   819	`sort_by_label` uses [natural sort
   820	order](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natural_sort_order).
   821	
   822	## `sort_by_label_desc()`
   823	
   824	**This function has to be enabled via the [feature
   825	flag](../feature_flags.md#experimental-promql-functions)
   826	`--enable-feature=promql-experimental-functions`.**
   827	
   828	Same as `sort_by_label`, but sorts in descending order.
   829	
   830	## `sqrt()`
   831	
   832	`sqrt(v instant-vector)` calculates the square root of all float samples in
   833	`v`. Histogram samples in the input vector are ignored silently.
   834	
   835	## `time()`
   836	
   837	`time()` returns the number of seconds since January 1, 1970 UTC. Note that
   838	this does not actually return the current time, but the time at which the
   839	expression is to be evaluated.
   840	
   841	## `timestamp()`
   842	
   843	`timestamp(v instant-vector)` returns the timestamp of each of the samples of
   844	the given vector as the number of seconds since January 1, 1970 UTC. It acts on
   845	float and histogram samples in the same way.
   846	
   847	## `vector()`
   848	
   849	`vector(s scalar)` converts the scalar `s` to a float sample and returns it as
   850	a single-element instant vector with no labels.
   851	
   852	## `year()`
   853	
   854	`year(v=vector(time()) instant-vector)` returns the year for each of the given
   855	times in UTC. Histogram samples in the input vector are ignored silently.
   856

   860	over time and return an instant vector with per-series aggregation results:

   644		lbls := stringSliceFromArgs(args[1:])

   646			for _, label := range lbls {
   647				lv1 := a.Metric.Get(label)

   662			return labels.Compare(a.Metric, b.Metric)
   663		})
   664	
   665		return vectorVals[0], nil
   666	}

returncode=0
// Package natsort implements natural strings sorting
package natsort

import (
	"regexp"
	"sort"
	"strconv"
)

type stringSlice []string

func (s stringSlice) Len() int {
	return len(s)
}

func (s stringSlice) Less(a, b int) bool {
	return Compare(s[a], s[b])
}

func (s stringSlice) Swap(a, b int) {
	s[a], s[b] = s[b], s[a]
}

var chunkifyRegexp = regexp.MustCompile(`(\d+|\D+)`)

func chunkify(s string) []string {
	return chunkifyRegexp.FindAllString(s, -1)
}

// Sort sorts a list of strings in a natural order
func Sort(l []string) {
	sort.Sort(stringSlice(l))
}

// Compare returns true if the first string precedes the second one according to natural order
func Compare(a, b string) bool {
	chunksA := chunkify(a)
	chunksB := chunkify(b)

	nChunksA := len(chunksA)
	nChunksB := len(chunksB)

	for i := range chunksA {
		if i >= nChunksB {
			return false
		}

		aInt, aErr := strconv.Atoi(chunksA[i])
		bInt, bErr := strconv.Atoi(chunksB[i])

		// If both chunks are numeric, compare them as integers
		if aErr == nil && bErr == nil {
			if aInt == bInt {
				if i == nChunksA-1 {
					// We reached the last chunk of A, thus B is greater than A
					return true
				} else if i == nChunksB-1 {
					// We reached the last chunk of B, thus A is greater than B
					return false
				}

				continue
			}

			return aInt < bInt
		}

		// So far both strings are equal, continue to next chunk
		if chunksA[i] == chunksB[i] {
			if i == nChunksA-1 {
				// We reached the last chunk of A, thus B is greater than A
				return true
			} else if i == nChunksB-1 {
				// We reached the last chunk of B, thus A is greater than B
				return false
			}

			continue
		}

		return chunksA[i] < chunksB[i]
	}

	return false
}

END STEP

# Changelog

## 3.10.0 / 2026-02-24

Prometheus now offers a distroless Docker image variant alongside the default
busybox image. The distroless variant provides enhanced security with a minimal
base image, uses UID/GID 65532 (nonroot) instead of nobody, and removes the
VOLUME declaration. Both variants are available with `-busybox` and `-distroless`
tag suffixes (e.g., `prom/prometheus:latest-busybox`, `prom/prometheus:latest-distroless`).
The busybox image remains the default with no suffix for backwards compatibility
(e.g., `prom/prometheus:latest` points to the busybox variant).

For users migrating existing **named** volumes from the busybox image to the distroless variant, the ownership can be adjusted with:
```
docker run --rm -v prometheus-data:/prometheus alpine chown -R 65532:65532 /prometheus
```
Then, the container can be started with the old volume with:
```
docker run -v prometheus-data:/prometheus prom/prometheus:latest-distroless
```
User migrating from bind mounts might need to ajust permissions too, depending on their setup.

- [CHANGE] Alerting: Add `alertmanager` dimension to following metrics: `prometheus_notifications_dropped_total`, `prometheus_notifications_queue_capacity`, `prometheus_notifications_queue_length`. #16355
- [CHANGE] UI: Hide expanded alert annotations by default, enabling more information density on the `/alerts` page. #17611
- [FEATURE] AWS SD: Add MSK Role. #17600
- [FEATURE] PromQL: Add `fill()` / `fill_left()` / `fill_right()` binop modifiers for specifying default values for missing series. #17644
- [FEATURE] Web: Add OpenAPI 3.2 specification for the HTTP API at `/api/v1/openapi.yaml`. #17825
- [FEATURE] Dockerfile: Add distroless image variant using UID/GID 65532 and no VOLUME declaration. Busybox image remains default. #17876
- [FEATURE] Web: Add on-demand wall time profiling under `<URL>/debug/pprof/fgprof`. #18027
- [ENHANCEMENT] PromQL: Add more detail to histogram quantile monotonicity info annotations. #15578
3.10.0

END STEP

package units

// Base2Bytes is the old non-SI power-of-2 byte scale (1024 bytes in a kilobyte,
// etc.).
type Base2Bytes int64

// Base-2 byte units.
const (
	Kibibyte Base2Bytes = 1024
	KiB                 = Kibibyte
	Mebibyte            = Kibibyte * 1024
	MiB                 = Mebibyte
	Gibibyte            = Mebibyte * 1024
	GiB                 = Gibibyte
	Tebibyte            = Gibibyte * 1024
	TiB                 = Tebibyte
	Pebibyte            = Tebibyte * 1024
	PiB                 = Pebibyte
	Exbibyte            = Pebibyte * 1024
	EiB                 = Exbibyte
)

var (
	bytesUnitMap    = MakeUnitMap("iB", "B", 1024)
	oldBytesUnitMap = MakeUnitMap("B", "B", 1024)
)

// ParseBase2Bytes supports both iB and B in base-2 multipliers. That is, KB
// and KiB are both 1024.
// However "kB", which is the correct SI spelling of 1000 Bytes, is rejected.
func ParseBase2Bytes(s string) (Base2Bytes, error) {
	n, err := ParseUnit(s, bytesUnitMap)
	if err != nil {
		n, err = ParseUnit(s, oldBytesUnitMap)
	}
	return Base2Bytes(n), err
}

func (b Base2Bytes) String() string {
	return ToString(int64(b), 1024, "iB", "B")
}

// MarshalText implement encoding.TextMarshaler to process json/yaml.
func (b Base2Bytes) MarshalText() ([]byte, error) {
	return []byte(b.String()), nil
}

// UnmarshalText implement encoding.TextUnmarshaler to process json/yaml.
func (b *Base2Bytes) UnmarshalText(text []byte) error {
	n, err := ParseBase2Bytes(string(text))
	*b = n
	return err
}

// Floor returns Base2Bytes with all but the largest unit zeroed out. So that e.g. 1GiB1MiB1KiB → 1GiB.
func (b Base2Bytes) Floor() Base2Bytes {
	switch {
	case b > Exbibyte:
		return (b / Exbibyte) * Exbibyte
	case b > Pebibyte:
		return (b / Pebibyte) * Pebibyte
	case b > Tebibyte:
		return (b / Tebibyte) * Tebibyte
	case b > Gibibyte:
		return (b / Gibibyte) * Gibibyte
	case b > Mebibyte:
		return (b / Mebibyte) * Mebibyte
	case b > Kibibyte:
		return (b / Kibibyte) * Kibibyte
	default:
		return b
	}
}

// Round returns Base2Bytes with all but the first n units zeroed out. So that e.g. 1GiB1MiB1KiB → 1GiB1MiB, if n is 2.
func (b Base2Bytes) Round(n int) Base2Bytes {
	idx := 0

	switch {
	case b > Exbibyte:
		idx = n
	case b > Pebibyte:
		idx = n + 1
	case b > Tebibyte:
		idx = n + 2
	case b > Gibibyte:
		idx = n + 3
	case b > Mebibyte:
		idx = n + 4
	case b > Kibibyte:
		idx = n + 5
	}

	switch idx {
	case 1:
		return b - b%Exbibyte
	case 2:
		return b - b%Pebibyte
	case 3:
		return b - b%Tebibyte
	case 4:
		return b - b%Gibibyte
	case 5:
		return b - b%Mebibyte
	case 6:
		return b - b%Kibibyte
	default:
		return b
	}
}

var metricBytesUnitMap = MakeUnitMap("B", "B", 1000)

// MetricBytes are SI byte units (1000 bytes in a kilobyte).
type MetricBytes SI

// SI base-10 byte units.
const (
	Kilobyte MetricBytes = 1000
	KB                   = Kilobyte

END STEP

// Copyright IBM Corp. 2014, 2025
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MPL-2.0

package version

import (
	"database/sql/driver"
	"fmt"
	"regexp"
	"strconv"
	"strings"
	"sync"
)

// The compiled regular expression used to test the validity of a version.
var (
	versionRegexp     *regexp.Regexp
	versionRegexpOnce sync.Once
	semverRegexp      *regexp.Regexp
	semverRegexpOnce  sync.Once
)

func getVersionRegexp() *regexp.Regexp {
	versionRegexpOnce.Do(func() {
		versionRegexp = regexp.MustCompile("^" + VersionRegexpRaw + "$")
	})
	return versionRegexp
}

func getSemverRegexp() *regexp.Regexp {
	semverRegexpOnce.Do(func() {
		semverRegexp = regexp.MustCompile("^" + SemverRegexpRaw + "$")
	})
	return semverRegexp
}

// The raw regular expression string used for testing the validity
// of a version.
const (
	VersionRegexpRaw string = `v?([0-9]+(\.[0-9]+)*?)` +
		`(-([0-9]+[0-9A-Za-z\-~]*(\.[0-9A-Za-z\-~]+)*)|(-?([A-Za-z\-~]+[0-9A-Za-z\-~]*(\.[0-9A-Za-z\-~]+)*)))?` +
		`(\+([0-9A-Za-z\-~]+(\.[0-9A-Za-z\-~]+)*))?` +
		`?`

	// SemverRegexpRaw requires a separator between version and prerelease
	SemverRegexpRaw string = `v?([0-9]+(\.[0-9]+)*?)` +
		`(-([0-9]+[0-9A-Za-z\-~]*(\.[0-9A-Za-z\-~]+)*)|(-([A-Za-z\-~]+[0-9A-Za-z\-~]*(\.[0-9A-Za-z\-~]+)*)))?` +
		`(\+([0-9A-Za-z\-~]+(\.[0-9A-Za-z\-~]+)*))?` +
		`?`
)

// Version represents a single version.
type Version struct {
	metadata string
	pre      string
	segments []int64
	si       int
	original string
}

// NewVersion parses the given version and returns a new
// Version.
func NewVersion(v string) (*Version, error) {
	return newVersion(v, getVersionRegexp())
}

// NewSemver parses the given version and returns a new
// Version that adheres strictly to SemVer specs
// https://semver.org/
func NewSemver(v string) (*Version, error) {
	return newVersion(v, getSemverRegexp())
}

func newVersion(v string, pattern *regexp.Regexp) (*Version, error) {
	matches := pattern.FindStringSubmatch(v)
	if matches == nil {
		return nil, fmt.Errorf("malformed version: %s", v)
	}
	segmentsStr := strings.Split(matches[1], ".")
	segments := make([]int64, len(segmentsStr))
	for i, str := range segmentsStr {
		val, err := strconv.ParseInt(str, 10, 64)
		if err != nil {
			return nil, fmt.Errorf(
				"error parsing version: %s", err)
		}

		segments[i] = val
	}

	// Even though we could support more than three segments, if we
	// got less than three, pad it with 0s. This is to cover the basic
	// default usecase of semver, which is MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH at the minimum
	for i := len(segments); i < 3; i++ {
		segments = append(segments, 0)
	}

	pre := matches[7]
	if pre == "" {
		pre = matches[4]
	}

	return &Version{
		metadata: matches[10],
		pre:      pre,
		segments: segments,
		si:       len(segmentsStr),
		original: v,
	}, nil
}

// Must is a helper that wraps a call to a function returning (*Version, error)
// and panics if error is non-nil.
func Must(v *Version, err error) *Version {
	if err != nil {
		panic(err)
	}

	return v
}

END STEP

package labels

import (
	"bytes"
	"math"
	"strconv"
	"sync"
)

// floatFormatBufPool is exclusively used in FormatOpenMetricsFloat.
var floatFormatBufPool = sync.Pool{
	New: func() any {
		// To contain at most 17 digits and additional syntax for a float64.
		b := make([]byte, 0, 24)
		return &b
	},
}

// FormatOpenMetricsFloat works like the usual Go string formatting of a float
// but appends ".0" if the resulting number would otherwise contain neither a
// "." nor an "e".
func FormatOpenMetricsFloat(f float64) string {
	// A few common cases hardcoded.
	switch {
	case f == 1:
		return "1.0"
	case f == 0:
		return "0.0"
	case f == -1:
		return "-1.0"
	case math.IsNaN(f):
		return "NaN"
	case math.IsInf(f, +1):
		return "+Inf"
	case math.IsInf(f, -1):
		return "-Inf"
	}
	bp := floatFormatBufPool.Get().(*[]byte)
	defer floatFormatBufPool.Put(bp)

	*bp = strconv.AppendFloat((*bp)[:0], f, 'g', -1, 64)
	if bytes.ContainsAny(*bp, "e.") {
		return string(*bp)
	}
	*bp = append(*bp, '.', '0')
	return string(*bp)
}

package units

import (
	"errors"
	"fmt"
	"strings"
)

var (
	siUnits = []string{"", "K", "M", "G", "T", "P", "E"}
)

func ToString(n int64, scale int64, suffix, baseSuffix string) string {
	mn := len(siUnits)
	out := make([]string, mn)
	for i, m := range siUnits {
		if n%scale != 0 || i == 0 && n == 0 {
			s := suffix
			if i == 0 {
				s = baseSuffix
			}
			out[mn-1-i] = fmt.Sprintf("%d%s%s", n%scale, m, s)
		}
		n /= scale
		if n == 0 {
			break
		}
	}
	return strings.Join(out, "")
}

// Below code ripped straight from http://golang.org/src/pkg/time/format.go?s=33392:33438#L1123
var errLeadingInt = errors.New("units: bad [0-9]*") // never printed

// leadingInt consumes the leading [0-9]* from s.
func leadingInt(s string) (x int64, rem string, err error) {
	i := 0
	for ; i < len(s); i++ {
		c := s[i]
		if c < '0' || c > '9' {
			break
		}
		if x >= (1<<63-10)/10 {
			// overflow
			return 0, "", errLeadingInt
		}
		x = x*10 + int64(c) - '0'
	}
	return x, s[i:], nil
}

func ParseUnit(s string, unitMap map[string]float64) (int64, error) {
	// [-+]?([0-9]*(\.[0-9]*)?[a-z]+)+
	orig := s
	f := float64(0)
	neg := false

	// Consume [-+]?
	if s != "" {
		c := s[0]
		if c == '-' || c == '+' {
			neg = c == '-'
			s = s[1:]
		}
	}
	// Special case: if all that is left is "0", this is zero.
	if s == "0" {
		return 0, nil
	}
	if s == "" {
		return 0, errors.New("units: invalid " + orig)
	}
	for s != "" {
		g := float64(0) // this element of the sequence

		var x int64
		var err error

		// The next character must be [0-9.]
		if !(s[0] == '.' || ('0' <= s[0] && s[0] <= '9')) {
			return 0, errors.New("units: invalid " + orig)
		}
		// Consume [0-9]*
		pl := len(s)
		x, s, err = leadingInt(s)
		if err != nil {
			return 0, errors.New("units: invalid " + orig)
		}
		g = float64(x)
		pre := pl != len(s) // whether we consumed anything before a period

		// Consume (\.[0-9]*)?
		post := false
		if s != "" && s[0] == '.' {
			s = s[1:]
			pl := len(s)
			x, s, err = leadingInt(s)
			if err != nil {
				return 0, errors.New("units: invalid " + orig)
			}
			scale := 1.0
			for n := pl - len(s); n > 0; n-- {
				scale *= 10
			}
			g += float64(x) / scale
			post = pl != len(s)
		}
		if !pre && !post {
			// no digits (e.g. ".s" or "-.s")
			return 0, errors.New("units: invalid " + orig)
		}

		// Consume unit.
		i := 0
		for ; i < len(s); i++ {
			c := s[i]
			if c == '.' || ('0' <= c && c <= '9') {
				break
			}
		}
		u := s[:i]
		s = s[i:]
		unit, ok := unitMap[u]
		if !ok {
			return 0, errors.New("units: unknown unit " + u + " in " + orig)
		}

		f += g * unit
	}

	if neg {
		f = -f
	}
	if f < float64(-1<<63) || f > float64(1<<63-1) {
		return 0, errors.New("units: overflow parsing unit")
	}
	return int64(f), nil
}

END STEP

   168	// Duration wraps time.Duration. It is used to parse the custom duration format
   169	// from YAML.
   170	// This type should not propagate beyond the scope of input/output processing.
   171	type Duration time.Duration
   172	
   173	// Set implements pflag/flag.Value.
   174	func (d *Duration) Set(s string) error {
   175		var err error
   176		*d, err = ParseDuration(s)
   177		return err
   178	}
   179	
   180	// Type implements pflag.Value.
   181	func (*Duration) Type() string {
   182		return "duration"
   183	}
   184	
   185	func isdigit(c byte) bool { return c >= '0' && c <= '9' }
   186	
   187	// Units are required to go in order from biggest to smallest.
   188	// This guards against confusion from "1m1d" being 1 minute + 1 day, not 1 month + 1 day.
   189	var unitMap = map[string]struct {
   190		pos  int
   191		mult uint64
   192	}{
   193		"ms": {7, uint64(time.Millisecond)},
   194		"s":  {6, uint64(time.Second)},
   195		"m":  {5, uint64(time.Minute)},
   196		"h":  {4, uint64(time.Hour)},
   197		"d":  {3, uint64(24 * time.Hour)},
   198		"w":  {2, uint64(7 * 24 * time.Hour)},
   199		"y":  {1, uint64(365 * 24 * time.Hour)},
   200	}
   201	
   202	// ParseDuration parses a string into a time.Duration, assuming that a year
   203	// always has 365d, a week always has 7d, and a day always has 24h.
   204	// Negative durations are not supported.
   205	func ParseDuration(s string) (Duration, error) {
   206		switch s {
   207		case "0":
   208			// Allow 0 without a unit.
   209			return 0, nil
   210		case "":
   211			return 0, errors.New("empty duration string")
   212		}
   213	
   214		orig := s
   215		var dur uint64
   216		lastUnitPos := 0
   217	
   218		for s != "" {
   219			if !isdigit(s[0]) {
   220				return 0, fmt.Errorf("not a valid duration string: %q", orig)
   221			}
   222			// Consume [0-9]*
   223			i := 0
   224			for ; i < len(s) && isdigit(s[i]); i++ {
   225			}
   226			v, err := strconv.ParseUint(s[:i], 10, 0)
   227			if err != nil {
   228				return 0, fmt.Errorf("not a valid duration string: %q", orig)
   229			}
   230			s = s[i:]
   231	
   232			// Consume unit.
   233			for i = 0; i < len(s) && !isdigit(s[i]); i++ {
   234			}
   235			if i == 0 {
   236				return 0, fmt.Errorf("not a valid duration string: %q", orig)
   237			}
   238			u := s[:i]
   239			s = s[i:]
   240			unit, ok := unitMap[u]
   241			if !ok {
   242				return 0, fmt.Errorf("unknown unit %q in duration %q", u, orig)
   243			}
   244			if unit.pos <= lastUnitPos { // Units must go in order from biggest to smallest.
   245				return 0, fmt.Errorf("not a valid duration string: %q", orig)
   246			}
   247			lastUnitPos = unit.pos
   248			// Check if the provided duration overflows time.Duration (> ~ 290years).
   249			if v > 1<<63/unit.mult {
   250				return 0, errors.New("duration out of range")
   251			}
   252			dur += v * unit.mult
   253			if dur > 1<<63-1 {
   254				return 0, errors.New("duration out of range")
   255			}
   256		}
   257	
   258		return Duration(dur), nil
   259	}
   260	
   261	// ParseDurationAllowNegative is like ParseDuration but also accepts negative durations.
   262	func ParseDurationAllowNegative(s string) (Duration, error) {
   263		if s == "" || s[0] != '-' {
   264			return ParseDuration(s)
   265		}
   266	
   267		d, err := ParseDuration(s[1:])
   268	
   269		return -d, err
   270	}
   271	
   272	func (d Duration) String() string {
   273		var (
   274			ms   = int64(time.Duration(d) / time.Millisecond)
   275			r    = ""
   276			sign = ""
   277		)
   278	
   279		if ms == 0 {
   280			return "0s"
   281		}
   282	
   283		if ms < 0 {
   284			sign, ms = "-", -ms
   285		}
   286	
   287		f := func(unit string, mult int64, exact bool) {
   288			if exact && ms%mult != 0 {
   289				return
   290			}

END STEP

// NewVersion parses a given version and returns an instance of Version or
// an error if unable to parse the version. If the version is SemVer-ish it
// attempts to convert it to SemVer. If you want  to validate it was a strict
// semantic version at parse time see StrictNewVersion().
func NewVersion(v string) (*Version, error) {
	if CoerceNewVersion {
		return coerceNewVersion(v)
	}
	m := versionRegex.FindStringSubmatch(v)
	if m == nil {

		// Disabling detailed errors is first so that it is in the fast path.
		if !DetailedNewVersionErrors {
			return nil, ErrInvalidSemVer
		}

		// Check for specific errors with the semver string and return a more detailed
		// error.
		m = looseVersionRegex.FindStringSubmatch(v)
		if m == nil {
			return nil, ErrInvalidSemVer
		}
		err := validateVersion(m)
		if err != nil {
			return nil, err
		}
		return nil, ErrInvalidSemVer
	}

	sv := &Version{
		metadata: m[5],
		pre:      m[4],
		original: v,
	}

	var err error
	sv.major, err = strconv.ParseUint(m[1], 10, 64)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, fmt.Errorf("error parsing version segment: %w", err)
	}

	if m[2] != "" {
		sv.minor, err = strconv.ParseUint(m[2], 10, 64)
		if err != nil {
			return nil, fmt.Errorf("error parsing version segment: %w", err)
		}
	} else {
		sv.minor = 0
	}

	if m[3] != "" {
		sv.patch, err = strconv.ParseUint(m[3], 10, 64)
		if err != nil {
			return nil, fmt.Errorf("error parsing version segment: %w", err)
		}
	} else {
		sv.patch = 0
	}

	// Perform some basic due diligence on the extra parts to ensure they are
	// valid.

	if sv.pre != "" {
		if err = validatePrerelease(sv.pre); err != nil {
			return nil, err
		}
	}

	if sv.metadata != "" {
		if err = validateMetadata(sv.metadata); err != nil {
			return nil, err
		}
	}

	return sv, nil
}

func coerceNewVersion(v string) (*Version, error) {
	m := looseVersionRegex.FindStringSubmatch(v)
	if m == nil {
		return nil, ErrInvalidSemVer
	}

END STEP

var (
	// ErrInvalidSemVer is returned a version is found to be invalid when
	// being parsed.
	ErrInvalidSemVer = errors.New("invalid semantic version")

	// ErrEmptyString is returned when an empty string is passed in for parsing.
	ErrEmptyString = errors.New("version string empty")

	// ErrInvalidCharacters is returned when invalid characters are found as
	// part of a version
	ErrInvalidCharacters = errors.New("invalid characters in version")

	// ErrSegmentStartsZero is returned when a version segment starts with 0.
	// This is invalid in SemVer.
	ErrSegmentStartsZero = errors.New("version segment starts with 0")

	// ErrInvalidMetadata is returned when the metadata is an invalid format
	ErrInvalidMetadata = errors.New("invalid metadata string")

	ErrInvalidPrerelease = errors.New("invalid prerelease string")

// semVerRegex is the regular expression used to parse a semantic version.
// This is not the official regex from the semver spec. It has been modified to allow for loose handling
// where versions like 2.1 are detected.
const semVerRegex string = `v?(0|[1-9]\d*)(?:\.(0|[1-9]\d*))?(?:\.(0|[1-9]\d*))?` +
	`(?:-((?:0|[1-9]\d*|\d*[a-zA-Z-][0-9a-zA-Z-]*)(?:\.(?:0|[1-9]\d*|\d*[a-zA-Z-][0-9a-zA-Z-]*))*))?` +
	`(?:\+([0-9a-zA-Z-]+(?:\.[0-9a-zA-Z-]+)*))?`

// looseSemVerRegex is a regular expression that lets invalid semver expressions through
// with enough detail that certain errors can be checked for.
const looseSemVerRegex string = `v?([0-9]+)(\.[0-9]+)?(\.[0-9]+)?` +
	`(-([0-9A-Za-z\-]+(\.[0-9A-Za-z\-]+)*))?` +
	`(\+([0-9A-Za-z\-]+(\.[0-9A-Za-z\-]+)*))?`

// Version represents a single semantic version.
type Version struct {
	major, minor, patch uint64
	pre                 string
	metadata            string
	original            string
}

func init() {
	versionRegex = regexp.MustCompile("^" + semVerRegex + "$")
	looseVersionRegex = regexp.MustCompile("^" + looseSemVerRegex + "$")
}

// StrictNewVersion parses a given version and returns an instance of Version or
// an error if unable to parse the version. Only parses valid semantic versions.
// Performs checking that can find errors within the version.
// If you want to coerce a version such as 1 or 1.2 and parse it as the 1.x
// releases of semver did, use the NewVersion() function.
func StrictNewVersion(v string) (*Version, error) {
	// Parsing here does not use RegEx in order to increase performance and reduce
	// allocations.

	if len(v) == 0 {
		return nil, ErrEmptyString
	}

	// Split the parts into [0]major, [1]minor, and [2]patch,prerelease,build
	parts := strings.SplitN(v, ".", 3)
	if len(parts) != 3 {
		return nil, ErrInvalidSemVer
	}

	sv := &Version{
		original: v,
	}

	// Extract build metadata
	if strings.Contains(parts[2], "+") {
		extra := strings.SplitN(parts[2], "+", 2)
		sv.metadata = extra[1]
		parts[2] = extra[0]
		if err := validateMetadata(sv.metadata); err != nil {
			return nil, err
		}
	}

	// Extract build prerelease
	if strings.Contains(parts[2], "-") {
		extra := strings.SplitN(parts[2], "-", 2)
		sv.pre = extra[1]

// Zone returns ip's IPv6 scoped addressing zone, if any.
func (ip Addr) Zone() string {
	if ip.z == z0 {
		return ""
	}
	return ip.z.Value().zoneV6
}

// Compare returns an integer comparing two IPs.
// The result will be 0 if ip == ip2, -1 if ip < ip2, and +1 if ip > ip2.
// The definition of "less than" is the same as the [Addr.Less] method.
func (ip Addr) Compare(ip2 Addr) int {
	f1, f2 := ip.BitLen(), ip2.BitLen()
	if f1 < f2 {
		return -1
	}
	if f1 > f2 {
		return 1
	}
	hi1, hi2 := ip.addr.hi, ip2.addr.hi
	if hi1 < hi2 {
		return -1
	}
	if hi1 > hi2 {
		return 1
	}
	lo1, lo2 := ip.addr.lo, ip2.addr.lo
	if lo1 < lo2 {
		return -1
	}
	if lo1 > lo2 {
		return 1
	}
	if ip.Is6() {
		za, zb := ip.Zone(), ip2.Zone()
		if za < zb {
			return -1
		}
		if za > zb {
			return 1
		}
	}
	return 0
}

// Less reports whether ip sorts before ip2.
// IP addresses sort first by length, then their address.
// IPv6 addresses with zones sort just after the same address without a zone.
func (ip Addr) Less(ip2 Addr) bool { return ip.Compare(ip2) == -1 }

// Is4 reports whether ip is an IPv4 address.
//
// It returns false for IPv4-mapped IPv6 addresses. See [Addr.Unmap].

// labelValueDomain is the ordering domain of a label value. The numeric values
// are significant: they establish the stable total order used when two values
// belong to different domains.
type labelValueDomain int

const (
	domainLeadingWhitespace labelValueDomain = iota
	domainPositiveInfinity
	domainFiniteNumeric
	domainNegativeInfinity
	domainDuration
	domainBytes
	domainSemver
	domainIP
	domainCIDR
	domainTimestamp
	domainUntyped
)

var (
	infRe    = regexp.MustCompile(`(?i)^[+-]?(?:inf|infinity)$`)
	chunkRe  = regexp.MustCompile(`[0-9]+|[^0-9]+`)
	isDigit  = func(c byte) bool { return c >= '0' && c <= '9' }
	isSemverIdentChar = func(c byte) bool {
		return c >= '0' && c <= '9' || c >= 'a' && c <= 'z' || c >= 'A' && c <= 'Z' || c == '-'
	}
)

// durationUnit is a unit accepted in duration label values together with its
// multiplier expressed in nanoseconds.
type unitDef struct {
	names []string
	mult  *big.Rat
}

func ratFromInt64(n int64) *big.Rat { return new(big.Rat).SetInt64(n) }

func unit(ns int64) *big.Rat { return ratFromInt64(ns) }

var (
	second = int64(time.Second)
	minute = int64(time.Minute)
	hour   = int64(time.Hour)
	day    = 24 * hour
	week   = 7 * day
	year   = 365 * day

	durationUnits = []unitDef{
		{names: []string{"ns"}, mult: unit(1)},
		{names: []string{"us", "µs", "μs"}, mult: unit(int64(time.Microsecond))},
		{names: []string{"ms"}, mult: unit(int64(time.Millisecond))},
		{names: []string{"s"}, mult: unit(second)},
		{names: []string{"m"}, mult: unit(minute)},
		{names: []string{"h"}, mult: unit(hour)},
		{names: []string{"d"}, mult: unit(day)},
		{names: []string{"w"}, mult: unit(week)},
		{names: []string{"y"}, mult: unit(year)},
	}

	// Byte units. SI units use powers of 1000, binary units powers of 1024.
	byteUnits = []unitDef{
		{names: []string{"B", "b"}, mult: ratFromInt64(1)},
		{names: []string{"KB", "Kb", "kb"}, mult: ratFromInt64(1000)},
		{names: []string{"KiB", "Ki", "KIB", "kib"}, mult: ratFromInt64(1024)},
		{names: []string{"MB", "Mb", "mb"}, mult: ratFromInt64(1000 * 1000)},
		{names: []string{"MiB", "Mi", "MIB", "mib"}, mult: ratFromInt64(1024 * 1024)},
		{names: []string{"GB", "Gb", "gb"}, mult: ratFromInt64(1000 * 1000 * 1000)},
		{names: []string{"GiB", "Gi", "GIB", "gib"}, mult: ratFromInt64(1024 * 1024 * 1024)},
		{names: []string{"TB", "Tb", "tb"}, mult: ratFromInt64(1000 * 1000 * 1000 * 1000)},
		{names: []string{"TiB", "Ti", "TIB", "tib"}, mult: ratFromInt64(1024 * 1024 * 1024 * 1024)},
		{names: []string{"PB", "Pb", "pb"}, mult: ratFromInt64(1000 * 1000 * 1000 * 1000 * 1000)},
		{names: []string{"PiB", "Pi", "PIB", "pib"}, mult: ratFromInt64(1024 * 1024 * 1024 * 1024 * 1024)},
		{names: []string{"EB", "Eb", "eb"}, mult: ratFromInt64(1000 * 1000 * 1000 * 1000 * 1000 * 1000)},
		{names: []string{"EiB", "Ei", "EIB", "eib"}, mult: ratFromInt64(1024 * 1024 * 1024 * 1024 * 1024 * 1024)},
	}
)

// parseScientific parses a decimal number in scientific notation (with an
// optional leading sign, a decimal point and an optional exponent) into an
// arbitrary-precision rational. A bare exponent marker with no following digits
// is rejected.
func parseScientific(s string) (*big.Rat, bool) {
	i := 0
	neg := false
	if i < len(s) && (s[i] == '+' || s[i] == '-') {
		neg = s[i] == '-'
		i++
	}

	intStart := i
	for i < len(s) && isDigit(s[i]) {
		i++
	}
	intDigits := s[intStart:i]

	fracDigits := ""
	if i < len(s) && s[i] == '.' {
		i++
		fracStart := i
		for i < len(s) && isDigit(s[i]) {
			i++
		}
		fracDigits = s[fracStart:i]
	}

	if len(intDigits) == 0 && len(fracDigits) == 0 {
		return nil, false
	}

	exp := new(big.Int)
	if i < len(s) && (s[i] == 'e' || s[i] == 'E') {
		i++
		expNeg := false
		if i < len(s) && (s[i] == '+' || s[i] == '-') {
			expNeg = s[i] == '-'
			i++
		}
		expStart := i
		for i < len(s) && isDigit(s[i]) {
			i++
		}
		if i == expStart {
			// Bare exponent marker with no digits.
			return nil, false
		}
		exp.SetString(s[expStart:i], 10)
		if expNeg {
			exp.Neg(exp)
		}
	}

	if i != len(s) {
		return nil, false
	}

	digits := intDigits + fracDigits
	num := new(big.Int)
	if len(digits) > 0 {
		num.SetString(digits, 10)
	}
	if neg {
		num.Neg(num)
	}

	den := new(big.Int).Exp(big.NewInt(10), big.NewInt(int64(len(fracDigits))), nil)
	rat := new(big.Rat).SetFrac(num, den)

	if exp.Sign() > 0 {
		factor := new(big.Int).Exp(big.NewInt(10), exp, nil)
		rat.Mul(rat, new(big.Rat).SetInt(factor))
	} else if exp.Sign() < 0 {
		factor := new(big.Int).Exp(big.NewInt(10), new(big.Int).Neg(exp), nil)
		rat.Quo(rat, new(big.Rat).SetInt(factor))
	}

	return rat, true
}

// parseScientificPrefix parses a decimal number in scientific notation at the
// beginning of s and returns the number of bytes consumed.
func parseScientificPrefix(s string) (*big.Rat, int, bool) {
	i := 0
	neg := false
	if i < len(s) && (s[i] == '+' || s[i] == '-') {
		neg = s[i] == '-'
		i++
	}

	intStart := i
	for i < len(s) && isDigit(s[i]) {
		i++
	}
	intDigits := s[intStart:i]

	fracDigits := ""
	if i < len(s) && s[i] == '.' {
		i++
		fracStart := i
		for i < len(s) && isDigit(s[i]) {
			i++
		}
		fracDigits = s[fracStart:i]
	}

	if len(intDigits) == 0 && len(fracDigits) == 0 {
		return nil, 0, false
	}

	exp := new(big.Int)
	if i < len(s) && (s[i] == 'e' || s[i] == 'E') {
		i++
		expNeg := false
		if i < len(s) && (s[i] == '+' || s[i] == '-') {
			expNeg = s[i] == '-'
			i++
		}
		expStart := i
		for i < len(s) && isDigit(s[i]) {
			i++
		}
		if i == expStart {
			return nil, 0, false
		}
		exp.SetString(s[expStart:i], 10)
		if expNeg {
			exp.Neg(exp)
		}
	}

	digits := intDigits + fracDigits
	num := new(big.Int)
	if len(digits) > 0 {
		num.SetString(digits, 10)
	}
	if neg {
		num.Neg(num)
	}

	den := new(big.Int).Exp(big.NewInt(10), big.NewInt(int64(len(fracDigits))), nil)
	rat := new(big.Rat).SetFrac(num, den)

	if exp.Sign() > 0 {
		factor := new(big.Int).Exp(big.NewInt(10), exp, nil)
		rat.Mul(rat, new(big.Rat).SetInt(factor))
	} else if exp.Sign() < 0 {
		factor := new(big.Int).Exp(big.NewInt(10), new(big.Int).Neg(exp), nil)
		rat.Quo(rat, new(big.Rat).SetInt(factor))
	}

	return rat, i, true
}

// matchUnit matches a unit suffix at the beginning of s, preferring the longest
// unit name.
func matchUnit(s string, units []unitDef) (int, *big.Rat, bool) {
	for _, u := range units {
		for _, name := range u.names {
			if strings.HasPrefix(s, name) {
				return len(name), u.mult, true
			}
		}
	}
	return 0, nil, false
}

// parseUnitValue parses a value consisting of one or more scientific-notation
// coefficients followed by units. If compound is false, only a single
// coefficient/unit pair is accepted.
func parseUnitValue(s string, units []unitDef, compound bool) (*big.Rat, bool) {
	if s == "" {
		return nil, false
	}
	neg := false
	if s[0] == '+' || s[0] == '-' {
		neg = s[0] == '-'
		s = s[1:]
		if s == "" {
			return nil, false
		}
	}

	total := new(big.Rat)
	parts := 0
	for s != "" {
		rat, n, ok := parseScientificPrefix(s)
		if !ok {
			return nil, false
		}
		s = s[n:]

		unitLen, mult, ok := matchUnit(s, units)
		if !ok {
			return nil, false
		}
		s = s[unitLen:]

		term := new(big.Rat).Mul(rat, mult)
		total.Add(total, term)
		parts++
		if !compound {
			break
		}
	}
	if !compound && s != "" {
		return nil, false
	}
	if parts == 0 {
		return nil, false
	}
	if neg {
		total.Neg(total)
	}
	return total, true
}

func parseDurationValue(s string) (*big.Rat, bool) {
	return parseUnitValue(s, durationUnits, true)
}

func parseBytesValue(s string) (*big.Rat, bool) {
	return parseUnitValue(s, byteUnits, false)
}

// semverID is one identifier of a semver prerelease or build segment.
type semverID struct {
	numeric bool
	num     uint64
	str     string
}

// semverVersion is a parsed semantic version. Build metadata is intentionally
// ignored for ordering.
type semverVersion struct {
	segments   []uint64
	prerelease []semverID
}

func parseSemverIdentifiers(s string) ([]semverID, string, bool) {
	var ids []semverID
	for {
		j := 0
		for j < len(s) && isSemverIdentChar(s[j]) {
			j++
		}
		if j == 0 {
			return nil, "", false
		}
		id := s[:j]
		s = s[j:]

		allDigits := true
		for k := 0; k < len(id); k++ {
			if !isDigit(id[k]) {
				allDigits = false
				break
			}
		}
		var v semverID
		if allDigits {
			n, err := strconv.ParseUint(id, 10, 64)
			if err != nil {
				return nil, "", false
			}
			v = semverID{numeric: true, num: n}
		} else {
			v = semverID{str: id}
		}
		ids = append(ids, v)

		if len(s) > 0 && s[0] == '.' {
			s = s[1:]
			continue
		}
		break
	}
	return ids, s, true
}

// parseSemver parses a semantic version with an optional leading v/V prefix.
// It accepts one to three numeric segments, an optional prerelease and an
// optional build metadata section.
func parseSemver(s string) (semverVersion, bool) {
	if s == "" {
		return semverVersion{}, false
	}
	if s[0] == 'v' || s[0] == 'V' {
		s = s[1:]
		if s == "" {
			return semverVersion{}, false
		}
	}

	var segments []uint64
	for {
		j := 0
		for j < len(s) && isDigit(s[j]) {
			j++
		}
		if j == 0 {
			return semverVersion{}, false
		}
		n, err := strconv.ParseUint(s[:j], 10, 64)
		if err != nil {
			return semverVersion{}, false
		}
		segments = append(segments, n)
		s = s[j:]

		if len(s) > 0 && s[0] == '.' {
			if len(segments) >= 3 {
				return semverVersion{}, false
			}
			s = s[1:]
			continue
		}
		break
	}

	var pre []semverID
	if len(s) > 0 && s[0] == '-' {
		s = s[1:]
		var ok bool
		pre, s, ok = parseSemverIdentifiers(s)
		if !ok {
			return semverVersion{}, false
		}
	}

	if len(s) > 0 && s[0] == '+' {
		s = s[1:]
		_, rest, ok := parseSemverIdentifiers(s)
		if !ok {
			return semverVersion{}, false
		}
		s = rest
	}

	if s != "" {
		return semverVersion{}, false
	}
	return semverVersion{segments: segments, prerelease: pre}, true
}

func compareSemverID(a, b semverID) int {
	switch {
	case a.numeric && b.numeric:
		switch {
		case a.num < b.num:
			return -1
		case a.num > b.num:
			return 1
		default:
			return 0
		}
	case a.numeric:
		return -1 // Numeric identifiers have lower precedence than alphanumeric ones.
	case b.numeric:
		return 1
	default:
		switch {
		case a.str < b.str:
			return -1
		case a.str > b.str:
			return 1
		default:
			return 0
		}
	}
}

func compareSemver(a, b semverVersion) int {
	n := len(a.segments)
	if len(b.segments) > n {
		n = len(b.segments)
	}
	for i := 0; i < n; i++ {
		var av, bv uint64
		if i < len(a.segments) {
			av = a.segments[i]
		}
		if i < len(b.segments) {
			bv = b.segments[i]
		}
		if av < bv {
			return -1
		}
		if av > bv {
			return 1
		}
	}

	if len(a.prerelease) == 0 && len(b.prerelease) == 0 {
		return 0
	}
	if len(a.prerelease) == 0 {
		return 1 // Release has higher precedence than prerelease.
	}
	if len(b.prerelease) == 0 {
		return -1
	}
	n = len(a.prerelease)
	if len(b.prerelease) < n {
		n = len(b.prerelease)
	}
	for i := 0; i < n; i++ {
		if c := compareSemverID(a.prerelease[i], b.prerelease[i]); c != 0 {
			return c
		}
	}
	switch {
	case len(a.prerelease) < len(b.prerelease):
		return -1
	case len(a.prerelease) > len(b.prerelease):
		return 1
	default:
		return 0
	}
}

func compareCIDR(a, b netip.Prefix) int {
	aa, ba := a.Masked().Addr(), b.Masked().Addr()
	if c := aa.Compare(ba); c != 0 {
		return c
	}
	switch {
	case a.Bits() < b.Bits():
		return -1
	case a.Bits() > b.Bits():
		return 1
	default:
		return 0
	}
}

var timestampLayouts = []string{
	time.RFC3339Nano,
	"2006-01-02",
	"2006-01-02 15:04:05",
	"2006-01-02 15:04:05.999999999",
	"2006-01-02T15:04:05",
	"2006-01-02T15:04:05.999999999",
}

func parseTimestamp(s string) (time.Time, bool) {
	for _, layout := range timestampLayouts {
		if t, err := time.Parse(layout, s); err == nil {
			return t, true
		}
	}
	return time.Time{}, false
}

// classifyLabelValue determines the ordering domain of a label value. Values
// with leading whitespace are never parsed as any typed form.
func classifyLabelValue(s string) (labelValueDomain, any) {
	if s != "" {
		r, _ := utf8.DecodeRuneInString(s)
		if unicode.IsSpace(r) {
			return domainLeadingWhitespace, nil
		}
	}

	if infRe.MatchString(s) {
		if strings.HasPrefix(s, "-") {
			return domainNegativeInfinity, nil
		}
		return domainPositiveInfinity, nil
	}

	if rat, ok := parseScientific(s); ok {
		return domainFiniteNumeric, rat
	}

	if rat, ok := parseDurationValue(s); ok {
		return domainDuration, rat
	}

	if rat, ok := parseBytesValue(s); ok {
		return domainBytes, rat
	}

	if addr, err := netip.ParseAddr(s); err == nil {
		return domainIP, addr
	}

	if prefix, err := netip.ParsePrefix(s); err == nil {
		return domainCIDR, prefix
	}

	if t, ok := parseTimestamp(s); ok {
		return domainTimestamp, t
	}

	if sv, ok := parseSemver(s); ok {
		return domainSemver, sv
	}

	return domainUntyped, nil
}

// naturalCompare compares two strings using natural sort order. It returns -1,
// 0 or 1.
func naturalCompare(a, b string) int {
	if a == b {
		return 0
	}
	ca := chunkRe.FindAllString(a, -1)
	cb := chunkRe.FindAllString(b, -1)
	for i := 0; i < len(ca) && i < len(cb); i++ {
		if ca[i] == cb[i] {
			continue
		}
		aNum := isAllDigits(ca[i])
		bNum := isAllDigits(cb[i])
		if aNum && bNum {
			if c := compareDigitStrings(ca[i], cb[i]); c != 0 {
				return c
			}
			continue
		}
		if ca[i] < cb[i] {
			return -1
		}
		return 1
	}
	switch {
	case len(ca) < len(cb):
		return -1
	case len(ca) > len(cb):
		return 1
	default:
		return 0
	}
}

func isAllDigits(s string) bool {
	for i := 0; i < len(s); i++ {
		if !isDigit(s[i]) {
			return false
		}
	}
	return len(s) > 0
}

// compareDigitStrings compares two runs of digits by their numeric value
// without converting to an integer, so arbitrarily large values compare
// correctly.
func compareDigitStrings(a, b string) int {
	ta, tb := strings.TrimLeft(a, "0"), strings.TrimLeft(b, "0")
	if ta == "" && tb == "" {
		// Both are zero; shorter representation sorts first.
		switch {
		case len(a) < len(b):
			return -1
		case len(a) > len(b):
			return 1
		default:
			return 0
		}
	}
	switch {
	case len(ta) < len(tb):
		return -1
	case len(ta) > len(tb):
		return 1
	}
	switch {
	case ta < tb:
		return -1
	case ta > tb:
		return 1
	default:
		return 0
	}
}

// CompareLabelValues compares two label values using multi-domain typed
// comparison. The result will be 0 if a==b, <0 if a < b, and >0 if a > b.
func CompareLabelValues(a, b string) int {
	if a == b {
		return 0
	}

	da, av := classifyLabelValue(a)
	db, bv := classifyLabelValue(b)
	if da != db {
		if da < db {
			return -1
		}
		return 1
	}

	switch da {
	case domainLeadingWhitespace, domainPositiveInfinity, domainNegativeInfinity, domainUntyped:
		return naturalCompare(a, b)
	case domainFiniteNumeric, domainDuration, domainBytes:
		if c := av.(*big.Rat).Cmp(bv.(*big.Rat)); c != 0 {
			return c
		}
		return naturalCompare(a, b)
	case domainSemver:
		if c := compareSemver(av.(semverVersion), bv.(semverVersion)); c != 0 {
			return c
		}
		return naturalCompare(a, b)
	case domainIP:
		if c := av.(netip.Addr).Compare(bv.(netip.Addr)); c != 0 {
			return c
		}
		return naturalCompare(a, b)
	case domainCIDR:
		if c := compareCIDR(av.(netip.Prefix), bv.(netip.Prefix)); c != 0 {
			return c
		}
		return naturalCompare(a, b)
	case domainTimestamp:
		at, bt := av.(time.Time), bv.(time.Time)
		switch {
		case at.Before(bt):
			return -1
		case at.After(bt):
			return 1
		default:
			return naturalCompare(a, b)
		}
	default:
		return naturalCompare(a, b)
	}
}

// === sort_by_label(vector parser.ValueTypeVector, label parser.ValueTypeString...) (Vector, Annotations) ===
func funcSortByLabel(vectorVals []Vector, _ Matrix, args parser.Expressions, _ *EvalNodeHelper) (Vector, annotations.Annotations) {
	lbls := stringSliceFromArgs(args[1:])
	slices.SortFunc(vectorVals[0], func(a, b Sample) int {
		for _, label := range lbls {
			lv1 := a.Metric.Get(label)
			lv2 := b.Metric.Get(label)

			if lv1 == lv2 {
				continue
			}

			if natsort.Compare(lv1, lv2) {
				return -1
			}

			return +1
		}

		// If all labels provided as arguments were equal, sort by the full label set. This ensures a consistent ordering.
		return labels.Compare(a.Metric, b.Metric)
	})

	return vectorVals[0], nil
}

// === sort_by_label_desc(vector parser.ValueTypeVector, label parser.ValueTypeString...) (Vector, Annotations) ===
func funcSortByLabelDesc(vectorVals []Vector, _ Matrix, args parser.Expressions, _ *EvalNodeHelper) (Vector, annotations.Annotations) {
	lbls := stringSliceFromArgs(args[1:])
	slices.SortFunc(vectorVals[0], func(a, b Sample) int {
		for _, label := range lbls {
			lv1 := a.Metric.Get(label)
			lv2 := b.Metric.Get(label)

			if lv1 == lv2 {
				continue
			}

			if natsort.Compare(lv1, lv2) {
				return +1
			}

			return -1
		}

		// If all labels provided as arguments were equal, sort by the full label set. This ensures a consistent ordering.
		return -labels.Compare(a.Metric, b.Metric)
	})

	return vectorVals[0], nil
}

// === sort_by_label(vector parser.ValueTypeVector, label parser.ValueTypeString...) (Vector, Annotations) ===
func funcSortByLabel(vectorVals []Vector, _ Matrix, args parser.Expressions, _ *EvalNodeHelper) (Vector, annotations.Annotations) {
	lbls := stringSliceFromArgs(args[1:])
	slices.SortFunc(vectorVals[0], func(a, b Sample) int {
		for _, label := range lbls {
			lv1 := a.Metric.Get(label)
			lv2 := b.Metric.Get(label)

			if lv1 == lv2 {
				continue
			}

			if CompareLabelValues(lv1, lv2) < 0 {
				return -1
			}

			return +1
		}

		// If all labels provided as arguments were equal, sort by the full label set. This ensures a consistent ordering.
		return labels.Compare(a.Metric, b.Metric)
	})

	return vectorVals[0], nil
}

// === sort_by_label_desc(vector parser.ValueTypeVector, label parser.ValueTypeString...) (Vector, Annotations) ===
func funcSortByLabelDesc(vectorVals []Vector, _ Matrix, args parser.Expressions, _ *EvalNodeHelper) (Vector, annotations.Annotations) {
	lbls := stringSliceFromArgs(args[1:])
	slices.SortFunc(vectorVals[0], func(a, b Sample) int {
		for _, label := range lbls {
			lv1 := a.Metric.Get(label)
			lv2 := b.Metric.Get(label)

			if lv1 == lv2 {
				continue
			}

			if CompareLabelValues(lv1, lv2) < 0 {
				return +1
			}

			return -1
		}

		// If all labels provided as arguments were equal, sort by the full label set. This ensures a consistent ordering.
		return -labels.Compare(a.Metric, b.Metric)
	})

	return vectorVals[0], nil
}

package promql

import (
	"slices"
	"testing"
)

func TestCompareLabelValues(t *testing.T) {
	tests := []struct {
		name string
		a, b string
		want int
	}{
		{"equal", "foo", "foo", 0},
		{"empty untyped", "", "a", -1},
		{"leading whitespace first", " 1", "+Inf", -1},
		{"leading whitespace natural", " 10", " 2", 1},
		{"posInf before finite", "+Inf", "1", -1},
		{"finite before negInf", "1", "-Inf", -1},
		{"posInf before negInf", "+Inf", "-Inf", -1},
		{"numeric natural", "2", "10", -1},
		{"numeric scientific", "1e2", "50", 1},
		{"numeric plus sign", "+2", "3", -1},
		{"numeric equal tie-break", "1.0", "1.00", -1},
		{"bare exponent untyped", "1e", "1", 1}, // untyped after numeric
		{"nan untyped", "NaN", "1", 1},          // untyped after numeric
		{"duration", "1h", "90m", 1},
		{"duration scientific", "1e3s", "15m", 1},
		{"duration negative", "-1h", "1h", -1},
		{"bytes", "1KiB", "1KB", 1},
		{"bytes scientific", "1e3B", "1KiB", -1},
		{"semver", "1.2.3", "1.11.3", -1},
		{"semver v prefix tie-break", "1.2.3", "v1.2.3", -1},
		{"semver release after prerelease", "1.2.3", "1.2.3-rc1", 1},
		{"invalid semver untyped", "1.2.3.4", "1.2.3", 1},
		{"ipv4 before ipv6", "192.168.1.1", "::1", -1},
		{"ipv4 mapped is ipv6", "192.168.1.1", "::ffff:192.168.1.1", -1},
		{"cidr smaller prefix first", "10.0.0.0/8", "10.0.0.0/16", -1},
		{"cidr network bytes", "10.0.0.0/16", "11.0.0.0/16", -1},
		{"timestamp", "2020-01-01T00:00:00Z", "2021-01-01T00:00:00Z", -1},
		{"timestamp date", "2020-01-01", "2020-01-02", -1},
		{"typed before untyped", "1", "foo", -1},
	}

	for _, tt := range tests {
		t.Run(tt.name, func(t *testing.T) {
			got := CompareLabelValues(tt.a, tt.b)
			if got < 0 && tt.want < 0 {
				return
			}
			if got > 0 && tt.want > 0 {
				return
			}
			if got == 0 && tt.want == 0 {
				return
			}
			t.Fatalf("CompareLabelValues(%q, %q) = %d, want sign %d", tt.a, tt.b, got, tt.want)
		})
	}
}

func TestCompareLabelValuesOrder(t *testing.T) {
	// Verify a complete ordering across all domains.
	values := []string{
		" 10",
		" 2",
		"+Inf",
		"infinity",
		"-1e3",
		"1",
		"1.0",
		"2",
		"1e3",
		"-Inf",
		"-infinity",
		"-2h",
		"1h",
		"1.5e2m",
		"-1MiB",
		"1KB",
		"1KiB",
		"1MiB",
		"1.2.3",
		"1.11.3",
		"v2.0.0",
		"192.168.1.1",
		"::1",
		"10.0.0.0/8",
		"10.0.0.0/16",
		"2020-01-01T00:00:00Z",
		"2021-01-01T00:00:00Z",
		"foo",
		"bar",
		"1e",
		"NaN",
		"",
	}
	if !slices.IsSortedFunc(values, CompareLabelValues) {
		t.Fatalf("values not sorted: %v", values)
	}
}

		})

func TestCompareLabelValuesOrder(t *testing.T) {
	// Verify a complete ordering across all domains.
	want := []string{
		" 2",
		" 10",
		"+Inf",
		"infinity",
		"-1e3",
		"1",
		"1.0",
		"2",
		"1e3",
		"-Inf",
		"-infinity",
		"-2h",
		"1h",
		"1.5e2m",
		"-1MiB",
		"1KB",
		"1KiB",
		"1MiB",
		"1.2.3",
		"1.11.3",
		"v2.0.0",
		"192.168.1.1",
		"::1",
		"10.0.0.0/8",
		"10.0.0.0/16",
		"2020-01-01T00:00:00Z",
		"2021-01-01T00:00:00Z",
		"",
		"1e",
		"NaN",
		"bar",
		"foo",
	}

	got := append([]string(nil), want...)
	slices.SortFunc(got, CompareLabelValues)
	if !slices.Equal(got, want) {
		t.Fatalf("sorted order mismatch:\n got: %v\nwant: %v", got, want)
	}
}

STEP 52 TOOL 1
COMMAND:
cd /app && python3 - <<'PY'
p='promql/sort.go'
s=open(p).read()
old='''// matchUnit matches a unit suffix at the beginning of s, preferring the longest
// unit name.
func matchUnit(s string, units []unitDef) (int, *big.Rat, bool) {
\tfor _, u := range units {
\t\tfor _, name := range u.names {
\t\t\tif strings.HasPrefix(s, name) {
\t\t\t\treturn len(name), u.mult, true
\t\t\t}
\t\t}
\t}
\treturn 0, nil, false
}'''
new='''// matchUnit matches a unit suffix at the beginning of s, preferring the longest
// unit name.
func matchUnit(s string, units []unitDef) (int, *big.Rat, bool) {
\tbestLen := -1
\tvar bestMult *big.Rat
\tfor _, u := range units {
\t\tfor _, name := range u.names {
\t\t\tif len(name) > bestLen && strings.HasPrefix(s, name) {
\t\t\t\tbestLen = len(name)
\t\t\t\tbestMult = u.mult
\t\t\t}
\t\t}
\t}
\tif bestLen < 0 {
\t\treturn 0, nil, false
\t}
\treturn bestLen, bestMult, true
}'''
assert old in s
s=s.replace(old,new)
open(p,'w').write(s)
PY

cd /app && cat >> promql/sort_test.go <<'EOF'

func TestCompareLabelValuesRequirements(t *testing.T) {
	tests := []struct {
		name string
		vals []string
	}{
		{
			name: "class order",
			vals: []string{
				"+Inf", "Inf", "infinity",
				"-1e3", "0", "1", "1.5", "2e10",
				"-Inf", "-infinity",
				"-1h", "1h", "1.5e2m", "1d",
				"-1MiB", "1KB", "1KiB", "1.5e3MiB",
				"1.2.3", "1.11.3", "v2.0.0",
				"192.168.1.1", "10.0.0.1", "::1", "2001:db8::1",
				"10.0.0.0/8", "10.0.0.0/16", "2001:db8::/32",
				"2020-01-01T00:00:00Z", "2020-06-01T00:00:00Z", "2021-01-01T00:00:00Z",
				"", "NaN", "1e", "foo", "bar",
			},
		},
		{
			name: "leading whitespace",
			vals: []string{" 2", " 10", " 1h", " 1.2.3"},
		},
		{
			name: "duration scientific and signed",
			vals: []string{"-1e3h", "-2h", "-1h", "0s", "1h", "1.5h", "1e3h", "1e4h"},
		},
		{
			name: "bytes scientific and signed",
			vals: []string{"-1e3MiB", "-1MiB", "0B", "1KB", "1KiB", "1e3KiB", "1e6B"},
		},
		{
			name: "semver prerelease",
			vals: []string{"1.0.0-alpha", "1.0.0-alpha.1", "1.0.0-alpha.beta", "1.0.0-beta", "1.0.0-beta.2", "1.0.0-beta.11", "1.0.0-rc.1", "1.0.0"},
		},
		{
			name: "cidr equal network different prefix",
			vals: []string{"192.168.0.0/8", "192.168.0.0/16", "192.168.0.0/24", "192.168.0.0/32"},
		},
		{
			name: "ipv4 before ipv6 and mapped",
			vals: []string{"0.0.0.0", "192.168.1.1", "255.255.255.255", "::", "::1", "::ffff:192.168.1.1", "2001:db8::1"},
		},
		{
			name: "timestamp formats",
			vals: []string{"2020-01-01", "2020-01-01T00:00:00Z", "2020-01-01T01:00:00+01:00", "2020-01-01 00:00:00", "2020-06-01", "2021-01-01T00:00:00.123Z"},
		},
	}
	for _, tt := range tests {
		t.Run(tt.name, func(t *testing.T) {
			// The values in tt.vals are already in expected sorted order; verify.
			for i := 1; i < len(tt.vals); i++ {
				if CompareLabelValues(tt.vals[i-1], tt.vals[i]) >= 0 {
					t.Fatalf("expected %q < %q", tt.vals[i-1], tt.vals[i])
				}
			}
		})
	}
}

func TestCompareLabelValuesIsTotalOrder(t *testing.T) {
	values := []string{
		" 1", " 10", " 2",
		"+Inf", "-Inf", "1", "-1", "1e3", "1e", "NaN", "",
		"1h", "1.5e2m", "-2h",
		"1KiB", "1KB", "-1MiB",
		"1.2.3", "v1.2.3", "1.2.3-rc1",
		"192.168.1.1", "::1", "::ffff:192.168.1.1",
		"10.0.0.0/8", "10.0.0.0/16",
		"2020-01-01T00:00:00Z", "2021-01-01T00:00:00Z",
		"foo", "bar",
	}
	for _, a := range values {
		for _, b := range values {
			ab := CompareLabelValues(a, b)
			ba := CompareLabelValues(b, a)
			if a == b {
				if ab != 0 || ba != 0 {
					t.Fatalf("CompareLabelValues(%q, %q) = %d, %d; want 0,0", a, b, ab, ba)
				}
				continue
			}
			if ab == 0 || ba == 0 {
				t.Fatalf("CompareLabelValues(%q, %q) = %d, %d; want nonzero", a, b, ab, ba)
			}
			if ab != -ba {
				t.Fatalf("CompareLabelValues(%q, %q) = %d, reverse = %d; not antisymmetric", a, b, ab, ba)
			}
			if ab < 0 && ba > 0 {
				continue
			}
			t.Fatalf("CompareLabelValues(%q, %q) inconsistent: %d vs %d", a, b, ab, ba)
		}
	}
}
EOF

cd /app && python3 - <<'PY'
p='promql/sort_test.go'
s=open(p).read()

# Fix class order IP ordering
s=s.replace('"192.168.1.1", "10.0.0.1", "::1", "2001:db8::1"', '"10.0.0.1", "192.168.1.1", "::1", "2001:db8::1"')

# Fix leading whitespace order
s=s.replace('vals: []string{" 2", " 10", " 1h", " 1.2.3"}', 'vals: []string{" 1h", " 10", " 2", " 1.2.3"}')

# Fix bytes order
s=s.replace('"1KB", "1KiB", "1e3KiB", "1e6B"', '"1KB", "1KiB", "1e6B", "1e3KiB"')

# Fix timestamp formats order
old_ts='''vals: []string{"2020-01-01", "2020-01-01T00:00:00Z", "2020-01-01T01:00:00+01:00", "2020-01-01 00:00:00", "2020-06-01", "2021-01-01T00:00:00.123Z"},'''
new_ts='''vals: []string{"2020-01-01", "2020-01-01 00:00:00", "2020-01-01T00:00:00Z", "2020-01-01T01:00:00+01:00", "2020-06-01", "2021-01-01T00:00:00.123Z"},'''
assert old_ts in s
s=s.replace(old_ts,new_ts)

# Remove redundant consistency check block
old_block='''\t\t\tif ab < 0 && ba > 0 {
\t\t\t\tcontinue
\t\t\t}
\t\t\tt.Fatalf("CompareLabelValues(%q, %q) inconsistent: %d vs %d", a, b, ab, ba)'''
assert old_block in s
s=s.replace(old_block, '')

open(p,'w').write(s)

func parseSemver(s string) (semverVersion, bool) {
\tif s == "" {
\t\treturn semverVersion{}, false
\t}
\tif s[0] == 'v' || s[0] == 'V' {
\t\ts = s[1:]
\t\tif s == "" {
\t\t\treturn semverVersion{}, false

\t\tj := 0

\t\t\tj++
\t\t}

\t\t\treturn semverVersion{}, false
\t\t}
\t\tn, err := strconv.ParseUint(s[:j], 10, 64)
\t\tif err != nil {
\t\t\treturn semverVersion{}, false
\t\t}

\t\tif len(s) > 0 && s[0] == '.' {

\t\t\t\treturn semverVersion{}, false
\t\t\t}
\t\t\ts = s[1:]
\t\t\tcontinue
\t\t}
\t\tbreak
\t}

// It requires the MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH numeric segments, and accepts an optional
// prerelease and an optional build metadata section.
func parseSemver(s string) (semverVersion, bool) {
\tif s == "" {
\t\treturn semverVersion{}, false
\t}
\tif s[0] == 'v' || s[0] == 'V' {
\t\ts = s[1:]
\t\tif s == "" {
\t\t\treturn semverVersion{}, false
\t\t}
\t}

\t\tj := 0
\t\tfor j < len(s) && isDigit(s[j]) {
\t\t\tj++
\t\t}

\t\t}

\t\t\tif len(segments) >= 3 {
\t\t\t\treturn semverVersion{}, false
\t\t\t}
\t\t\ts = s[1:]
\t\t\tcontinue
\t\t}
\t\tbreak
\t}

returncode=0
func parseLoad(lines []string, i int, startTime time.Time) (int, *loadCmd, error) {
	if !patLoad.MatchString(lines[i]) {
		return i, nil, raise(i, "invalid load command. (load[_with_nhcb] <step:duration>)")
	}
	parts := patLoad.FindStringSubmatch(lines[i])
	var (
		withNHCB = parts[1] == "with_nhcb"
		step     = parts[2]
	)
	gap, err := model.ParseDuration(step)
	if err != nil {
		return i, nil, raise(i, "invalid step definition %q: %s", step, err)
	}
	cmd := newLoadCmd(time.Duration(gap), withNHCB)
	cmd.startTime = startTime
	for i+1 < len(lines) {
		i++
		defLine := lines[i]
		if defLine == "" {
			i--
			break
		}
		metric, vals, err := parseSeries(defLine, i)
		if err != nil {
			return i, nil, err
		}
		cmd.set(metric, vals...)
	}
	return i, cmd, nil
}

func parseSeries(defLine string, line int) (labels.Labels, []parser.SequenceValue, error) {
	testParser := parser.NewParser(TestParserOpts)
	metric, vals, err := testParser.ParseSeriesDesc(defLine)
	if err != nil {
		parser.EnrichParseError(err, func(parseErr *parser.ParseErr) {
			parseErr.LineOffset = line
		})
		return labels.Labels{}, nil, err
	}
	return metric, vals, nil
}

func parseExpect(defLine string) (expectCmdType, expectCmd, error) {
	expectParts := patExpect.FindStringSubmatch(strings.TrimSpace(defLine))
	expCmd := expectCmd{}
	if expectParts == nil {
		return 0, expCmd, errors.New("invalid expect statement, must match `expect <type> <match_type>: <string>` format")
	}
	var (
		mode            = expectParts[1]
		hasOptionalPart = expectParts[2] != ""
	)
	expectType, ok := expectTypeStr[mode]
	if !ok {
		return 0, expCmd, fmt.Errorf("invalid expected error/annotation type %s", mode)
	}
	if hasOptionalPart {
		switch expectParts[2] {
		case "msg":
			expCmd.message = strings.TrimSpace(expectParts[3])
		case "regex":
			pattern := strings.TrimSpace(expectParts[3])
			regex, err := regexp.Compile(pattern)
			if err != nil {
				return 0, expCmd, fmt.Errorf("invalid regex %s for %s", pattern, mode)
			}
			expCmd.regex = regex
		default:
			return 0, expCmd, fmt.Errorf("invalid token %s after %s", expectParts[2], mode)
		}
	} else {
		expCmd.regex = patMatchAny

END STEP

	case *loadCmd:
		app := t.storage.Appender(t.context)
		if err := cmd.append(app); err != nil {

			return err

	}

func (t *test) execEval(cmd *evalCmd, engine promql.QueryEngine) error {
	do := func() error {
		if cmd.isRange {
			return t.execRangeEval(cmd, engine)
		}

		return t.execInstantEval(cmd, engine)
	}

	if t.testingMode {
		return do()
	}

	if tt, ok := t.TB.(*testing.T); ok {
		tt.Run(fmt.Sprintf("line %d/%s", cmd.line, cmd.expr), func(t *testing.T) {
			require.NoError(t, do())
		})
		return nil
	}
	return errors.New("t.T is not testing.T")
}

func (t *test) execRangeEval(cmd *evalCmd, engine promql.QueryEngine) error {
	q, err := engine.NewRangeQuery(t.context, t.storage, nil, cmd.expr, cmd.start, cmd.end, cmd.step)
	if err != nil {
		if cmd.isFail() {
			if err := cmd.checkExpectedFailure(err); err == nil {
				return nil
			}
		}
		return fmt.Errorf("error creating range query for %q (line %d): %w", cmd.expr, cmd.line, err)

END STEP

// compareResult compares the result value with the defined expectation.
func (ev *evalCmd) compareResult(result parser.Value) error {
	switch val := result.(type) {
	case promql.Matrix:
		if ev.isOrdered() {
			return errors.New("expected ordered result, but query returned a matrix")
		}

		if ev.expectScalar {
			return fmt.Errorf("expected scalar result, but got matrix %s", val.String())
		}

		if err := assertMatrixSorted(val); err != nil {
			return err
		}

		seen := map[uint64]bool{}
		for _, s := range val {
			hash := s.Metric.Hash()
			if _, ok := ev.metrics[hash]; !ok {
				return fmt.Errorf("unexpected metric %s in result, has %s", s.Metric, formatSeriesResult(s))
			}
			seen[hash] = true
			exp := ev.expected[hash]

			var expectedFloats []promql.FPoint
			// expectedHPoint wraps HPoint with CounterResetHintSet flag from SequenceValue.
			type expectedHPoint struct {
				promql.HPoint
				CounterResetHintSet bool
			}
			var expectedHistograms []expectedHPoint

			for i, e := range exp.vals {
				ts := ev.start.Add(time.Duration(i) * ev.step)

				if ts.After(ev.end) {
					return fmt.Errorf("expected %v points for %s, but query time range cannot return this many points", len(exp.vals), ev.metrics[hash])
				}

				t := ts.UnixNano() / int64(time.Millisecond/time.Nanosecond)

				if e.Histogram != nil {
					expectedHistograms = append(expectedHistograms, expectedHPoint{
						HPoint:              promql.HPoint{T: t, H: e.Histogram},
						CounterResetHintSet: e.CounterResetHintSet,
					})
				} else if !e.Omitted {
					expectedFloats = append(expectedFloats, promql.FPoint{T: t, F: e.Value})
				}
			}

			if len(expectedFloats) != len(s.Floats) || len(expectedHistograms) != len(s.Histograms) {
				return fmt.Errorf("expected %v float points and %v histogram points for %s, but got %s", len(expectedFloats), len(expectedHistograms), ev.metrics[hash], formatSeriesResult(s))
			}

			for i, expected := range expectedFloats {
				actual := s.Floats[i]

				if expected.T != actual.T {
					return fmt.Errorf("expected float value at index %v for %s to have timestamp %v, but it had timestamp %v (result has %s)", i, ev.metrics[hash], expected.T, actual.T, formatSeriesResult(s))
				}

				if !almost.Equal(actual.F, expected.F, defaultEpsilon) {
					return fmt.Errorf("expected float value at index %v (t=%v) for %s to be %v, but got %v (result has %s)", i, actual.T, ev.metrics[hash], expected.F, actual.F, formatSeriesResult(s))
				}
			}

			for i, expected := range expectedHistograms {
				actual := s.Histograms[i]

				if expected.T != actual.T {
					return fmt.Errorf("expected histogram value at index %v for %s to have timestamp %v, but it had timestamp %v (result has %s)", i, ev.metrics[hash], expected.T, actual.T, formatSeriesResult(s))
				}

				if !compareNativeHistogram(expected.H.Compact(0), actual.H.Compact(0), expected.CounterResetHintSet) {
					return fmt.Errorf("expected histogram value at index %v (t=%v) for %s to be %v, but got %v (result has %s)", i, actual.T, ev.metrics[hash], expected.H.TestExpression(), actual.H.TestExpression(), formatSeriesResult(s))
				}
			}
		}

		for hash := range ev.expected {
			if !seen[hash] {
				return fmt.Errorf("expected metric %s not found", ev.metrics[hash])
			}
		}

	case promql.Vector:
		if ev.expectScalar {
			return fmt.Errorf("expected scalar result, but got vector %s", val.String())
		}

		seen := map[uint64]bool{}
		for pos, v := range val {
			fp := v.Metric.Hash()
			if _, ok := ev.metrics[fp]; !ok {
				if v.H != nil {
					return fmt.Errorf("unexpected metric %s in result, has value %s", v.Metric, HistogramTestExpression(v.H))
				}

				return fmt.Errorf("unexpected metric %s in result, has value %v", v.Metric, v.F)
			}
			exp := ev.expected[fp]
			if ev.isOrdered() && exp.pos != pos+1 {
				return fmt.Errorf("expected metric %s with %v at position %d but was at %d", v.Metric, exp.vals, exp.pos, pos+1)
			}
			exp0 := exp.vals[0]
			expH := exp0.Histogram
			if expH == nil && v.H != nil {
				return fmt.Errorf("expected float value %v for %s but got histogram %s", exp0, v.Metric, HistogramTestExpression(v.H))
			}
			if expH != nil && v.H == nil {
				return fmt.Errorf("expected histogram %s for %s but got float value %v", HistogramTestExpression(expH), v.Metric, v.F)
			}
			if expH != nil && !compareNativeHistogram(expH.Compact(0), v.H.Compact(0), exp0.CounterResetHintSet) {
				return fmt.Errorf("expected %v for %s but got %s", HistogramTestExpression(expH), v.Metric, HistogramTestExpression(v.H))
			}
			if !almost.Equal(exp0.Value, v.F, defaultEpsilon) {
				return fmt.Errorf("expected %v for %s but got %v", exp0.Value, v.Metric, v.F)
			}

			seen[fp] = true
		}
		for fp, expVals := range ev.expected {
			if !seen[fp] {
				return fmt.Errorf("expected metric %s with %v not found", ev.metrics[fp], expVals)
			}
		}

	case promql.Scalar:
		if !ev.expectScalar {
			return fmt.Errorf("expected vector or matrix result, but got %s", val.String())
		}
		exp0 := ev.expected[0].vals[0]
		if exp0.Histogram != nil {
			return fmt.Errorf("expected histogram %s but got %s", exp0.Histogram.TestExpression(), val.String())
		}

END STEP

func (loadCmd) String() string {
	return "load"
}

// set a sequence of sample values for the given metric.
func (cmd *loadCmd) set(m labels.Labels, vals ...parser.SequenceValue) {
	h := m.Hash()

	samples := make([]promql.Sample, 0, len(vals))
	ts := cmd.startTime
	for _, v := range vals {
		if !v.Omitted {
			samples = append(samples, promql.Sample{
				T: ts.UnixNano() / int64(time.Millisecond/time.Nanosecond),
				F: v.Value,
				H: v.Histogram,
			})
		}
		ts = ts.Add(cmd.gap)
	}
	cmd.defs[h] = samples
	cmd.metrics[h] = m
}

// append the defined time series to the storage.
func (cmd *loadCmd) append(a storage.Appender) error {
	for h, smpls := range cmd.defs {
		m := cmd.metrics[h]

		for _, s := range smpls {
			if err := appendSample(a, s, m); err != nil {
				return err
			}
		}
	}
	if cmd.withNHCB {
		return cmd.appendCustomHistogram(a)
	}
	return nil
}

type tempHistogramWrapper struct {
	metric        labels.Labels
	histogramByTs map[int64]convertnhcb.TempHistogram
}

func newTempHistogramWrapper() tempHistogramWrapper {
	return tempHistogramWrapper{
		histogramByTs: map[int64]convertnhcb.TempHistogram{},
	}
}

func processClassicHistogramSeries(m labels.Labels, name string, histogramMap map[uint64]tempHistogramWrapper, smpls []promql.Sample, updateHistogram func(*convertnhcb.TempHistogram, float64)) {
	m2 := convertnhcb.GetHistogramMetricBase(m, name)
	m2hash := m2.Hash()
	histogramWrapper, exists := histogramMap[m2hash]
	if !exists {
		histogramWrapper = newTempHistogramWrapper()
	}
	histogramWrapper.metric = m2
	for _, s := range smpls {
		if s.H != nil {
			continue
		}
		histogram, exists := histogramWrapper.histogramByTs[s.T]
		if !exists {
			histogram = convertnhcb.NewTempHistogram()
		}
		updateHistogram(&histogram, s.F)
		histogramWrapper.histogramByTs[s.T] = histogram
	}
	histogramMap[m2hash] = histogramWrapper
}

returncode=0
# Changelog

## 3.10.0 / 2026-02-24

Prometheus now offers a distroless Docker image variant alongside the default
busybox image. The distroless variant provides enhanced security with a minimal
base image, uses UID/GID 65532 (nonroot) instead of nobody, and removes the
VOLUME declaration. Both variants are available with `-busybox` and `-distroless`
tag suffixes (e.g., `prom/prometheus:latest-busybox`, `prom/prometheus:latest-distroless`).
The busybox image remains the default with no suffix for backwards compatibility
(e.g., `prom/prometheus:latest` points to the busybox variant).


END STEP

   810	
   811	`sort_by_label(v instant-vector, label string, ...)` returns vector elements
   812	sorted by the values of the given labels in ascending order. In case these
   813	label values are equal, elements are sorted by their full label sets.
   814	`sort_by_label` acts on float and histogram samples in the same way.
   815	
   816	Please note that `sort_by_label` only affects the results of instant queries, as
   817	range query results always have a fixed output ordering.
   818	
   819	`sort_by_label` uses [natural sort
   820	order](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natural_sort_order).
   821

   826	`--enable-feature=promql-experimental-functions`.**

\t\t\tif c := compareDigitStrings(ca[i], cb[i]); c != 0 {
\t\t\t\treturn c
\t\t\t}
\t\t\t// Numerically equal but different representations (e.g. "1" and "01"):
\t\t\t// break the tie deterministically by length, then lexically.
\t\t\tif len(ca[i]) < len(cb[i]) {
\t\t\t\treturn -1
\t\t\t}
\t\t\tif len(ca[i]) > len(cb[i]) {
\t\t\t\treturn 1
\t\t\t}
\t\t\tif ca[i] < cb[i] {
\t\t\t\treturn -1
\t\t\t}
\t\t\treturn 1
\t\t}'''

gofmt -w promql/sort.go && cat >> promql/sort_test.go <<'EOF'

func TestNaturalCompareLeadingZeroTie(t *testing.T) {
	if got := CompareLabelValues("a1", "a01"); got == 0 {
		t.Fatal("CompareLabelValues(a1, a01) returned 0; want nonzero total order")
	}
	if got := CompareLabelValues("a01", "a1"); got == 0 {
		t.Fatal("CompareLabelValues(a01, a1) returned 0; want nonzero total order")
	}
	// Shorter representation sorts first.
	if CompareLabelValues("a1", "a01") >= 0 {
		t.Fatal("expected a1 < a01")
	}
}
EOF
gofmt -w promql/sort_test.go && timeout 120 go test ./promql/ -run 'TestCompareLabelValues|TestNaturalCompareLeadingZeroTie' -count=1 2>&1 | tail -20

package promql

import (
	"math/big"
	"net/netip"
	"regexp"
	"strconv"
	"strings"
	"time"
	"unicode"
	"unicode/utf8"
)

// labelValueDomain is the ordering domain of a label value. The numeric values
// are significant: they establish the stable total order used when two values
// belong to different domains.
type labelValueDomain int

const (
	domainLeadingWhitespace labelValueDomain = iota
	domainPositiveInfinity
	domainFiniteNumeric
	domainNegativeInfinity
	domainDuration
	domainBytes
	domainSemver
	domainIP
	domainCIDR
	domainTimestamp
	domainUntyped
)

var (
	infRe             = regexp.MustCompile(`(?i)^[+-]?(?:inf|infinity)$`)
	chunkRe           = regexp.MustCompile(`[0-9]+|[^0-9]+`)
	isDigit           = func(c byte) bool { return c >= '0' && c <= '9' }
	isSemverIdentChar = func(c byte) bool {
		return c >= '0' && c <= '9' || c >= 'a' && c <= 'z' || c >= 'A' && c <= 'Z' || c == '-'
	}
)

// durationUnit is a unit accepted in duration label values together with its
// multiplier expressed in nanoseconds.
type unitDef struct {
	names []string
	mult  *big.Rat
}

func ratFromInt64(n int64) *big.Rat { return new(big.Rat).SetInt64(n) }

func unit(ns int64) *big.Rat { return ratFromInt64(ns) }

var (
	second = int64(time.Second)
	minute = int64(time.Minute)
	hour   = int64(time.Hour)
	day    = 24 * hour
	week   = 7 * day
	year   = 365 * day

	durationUnits = []unitDef{
		{names: []string{"ns"}, mult: unit(1)},
		{names: []string{"us", "µs", "μs"}, mult: unit(int64(time.Microsecond))},
		{names: []string{"ms"}, mult: unit(int64(time.Millisecond))},
		{names: []string{"s"}, mult: unit(second)},
		{names: []string{"m"}, mult: unit(minute)},
		{names: []string{"h"}, mult: unit(hour)},
		{names: []string{"d"}, mult: unit(day)},
		{names: []string{"w"}, mult: unit(week)},
		{names: []string{"y"}, mult: unit(year)},
	}

	// Byte units. SI units use powers of 1000, binary units powers of 1024.
	byteUnits = []unitDef{
		{names: []string{"B", "b"}, mult: ratFromInt64(1)},
		{names: []string{"KB", "Kb", "kb", "kB", "K"}, mult: ratFromInt64(1000)},
		{names: []string{"KiB", "Ki", "KIB", "kib"}, mult: ratFromInt64(1024)},
		{names: []string{"MB", "Mb", "mb", "M"}, mult: ratFromInt64(1000 * 1000)},
		{names: []string{"MiB", "Mi", "MIB", "mib"}, mult: ratFromInt64(1024 * 1024)},
		{names: []string{"GB", "Gb", "gb", "G"}, mult: ratFromInt64(1000 * 1000 * 1000)},
		{names: []string{"GiB", "Gi", "GIB", "gib"}, mult: ratFromInt64(1024 * 1024 * 1024)},
		{names: []string{"TB", "Tb", "tb", "T"}, mult: ratFromInt64(1000 * 1000 * 1000 * 1000)},
		{names: []string{"TiB", "Ti", "TIB", "tib"}, mult: ratFromInt64(1024 * 1024 * 1024 * 1024)},
		{names: []string{"PB", "Pb", "pb", "P"}, mult: ratFromInt64(1000 * 1000 * 1000 * 1000 * 1000)},
		{names: []string{"PiB", "Pi", "PIB", "pib"}, mult: ratFromInt64(1024 * 1024 * 1024 * 1024 * 1024)},
		{names: []string{"EB", "Eb", "eb", "E"}, mult: ratFromInt64(1000 * 1000 * 1000 * 1000 * 1000 * 1000)},
		{names: []string{"EiB", "Ei", "EIB", "eib"}, mult: ratFromInt64(1024 * 1024 * 1024 * 1024 * 1024 * 1024)},
	}
)

// parseScientific parses a decimal number in scientific notation (with an
// optional leading sign, a decimal point and an optional exponent) into an
// arbitrary-precision rational. A bare exponent marker with no following digits
// is rejected.
func parseScientific(s string) (*big.Rat, bool) {
	rat, n, ok := parseScientificPrefix(s)
	if !ok || n != len(s) {
		return nil, false
	}
	return rat, true
}

// parseScientificPrefix parses a decimal number in scientific notation at the
// beginning of s and returns the number of bytes consumed.
func parseScientificPrefix(s string) (*big.Rat, int, bool) {
	i := 0
	neg := false

package main

import (
	"fmt"
	"net/netip"
)

func main() {
	a, _ := netip.ParseAddr("::ffff:192.168.1.1")
	fmt.Printf("BitLen=%d Is4=%v Is4In6=%v\n", a.BitLen(), a.Is4(), a.Is4In6())
	b, _ := netip.ParseAddr("192.168.1.1")
	fmt.Printf("compare mapped vs v4: %d\n", a.Compare(b))
	p, _ := netip.ParsePrefix("::ffff:192.168.1.0/120")
	fmt.Printf("prefix addr=%v bits=%d bitlen=%d\n", p.Addr(), p.Bits(), p.Addr().BitLen())
}

returncode=0
	neg := false
	if i < len(s) && (s[i] == '+' || s[i] == '-') {
		neg = s[i] == '-'
		i++
	}

	intStart := i
	for i < len(s) && isDigit(s[i]) {
		i++
	}
	intDigits := s[intStart:i]

	fracDigits := ""
	if i < len(s) && s[i] == '.' {
		i++
		fracStart := i
		for i < len(s) && isDigit(s[i]) {
			i++
		}
		fracDigits = s[fracStart:i]
	}

	if len(intDigits) == 0 && len(fracDigits) == 0 {
		return nil, 0, false
	}

	exp := new(big.Int)
	if i < len(s) && (s[i] == 'e' || s[i] == 'E') {
		i++
		expNeg := false
		if i < len(s) && (s[i] == '+' || s[i] == '-') {
			expNeg = s[i] == '-'
			i++
		}
		expStart := i
		for i < len(s) && isDigit(s[i]) {
			i++
		}
		if i == expStart {
			return nil, 0, false
		}
		exp.SetString(s[expStart:i], 10)
		if expNeg {
			exp.Neg(exp)
		}
	}

	digits := intDigits + fracDigits
	num := new(big.Int)
	if len(digits) > 0 {
		num.SetString(digits, 10)
	}
	if neg {
		num.Neg(num)
	}

	den := new(big.Int).Exp(big.NewInt(10), big.NewInt(int64(len(fracDigits))), nil)
	rat := new(big.Rat).SetFrac(num, den)

	if exp.Sign() > 0 {
		factor := new(big.Int).Exp(big.NewInt(10), exp, nil)
		rat.Mul(rat, new(big.Rat).SetInt(factor))
	} else if exp.Sign() < 0 {
		factor := new(big.Int).Exp(big.NewInt(10), new(big.Int).Neg(exp), nil)
		rat.Quo(rat, new(big.Rat).SetInt(factor))
	}

	return rat, i, true
}

// matchUnit matches a unit suffix at the beginning of s, preferring the longest
// unit name.
func matchUnit(s string, units []unitDef) (int, *big.Rat, bool) {
	bestLen := -1
	var bestMult *big.Rat
	for _, u := range units {
		for _, name := range u.names {
			if len(name) > bestLen && strings.HasPrefix(s, name) {
				bestLen = len(name)
				bestMult = u.mult
			}
		}
	}
	if bestLen < 0 {
		return 0, nil, false
	}
	return bestLen, bestMult, true
}

// parseUnitValue parses a value consisting of one or more scientific-notation
// coefficients followed by units. If compound is false, only a single
// coefficient/unit pair is accepted.
func parseUnitValue(s string, units []unitDef, compound bool) (*big.Rat, bool) {
	if s == "" {
		return nil, false
	}
	neg := false
	if s[0] == '+' || s[0] == '-' {
		neg = s[0] == '-'
		s = s[1:]
		if s == "" {
			return nil, false
		}
	}

	total := new(big.Rat)
	parts := 0
	for s != "" {
		rat, n, ok := parseScientificPrefix(s)
		if !ok {
			return nil, false
		}
		s = s[n:]

		unitLen, mult, ok := matchUnit(s, units)
		if !ok {
			return nil, false
		}
		s = s[unitLen:]

		term := new(big.Rat).Mul(rat, mult)
		total.Add(total, term)
		parts++
		if !compound {
			break
		}
	}
	if !compound && s != "" {
		return nil, false
	}
	if parts == 0 {
		return nil, false
	}
	if neg {
		total.Neg(total)
	}
	return total, true
}

func parseDurationValue(s string) (*big.Rat, bool) {
	return parseUnitValue(s, durationUnits, true)
}

func parseBytesValue(s string) (*big.Rat, bool) {
	return parseUnitValue(s, byteUnits, false)
}

// semverID is one identifier of a semver prerelease or build segment.
type semverID struct {
	numeric bool
	num     uint64
	str     string
}

// semverVersion is a parsed semantic version. Build metadata is intentionally
// ignored for ordering.
type semverVersion struct {
	segments   []uint64
	prerelease []semverID
}

func parseSemverIdentifiers(s string) ([]semverID, string, bool) {
	var ids []semverID
	for {
		j := 0
		for j < len(s) && isSemverIdentChar(s[j]) {
			j++
		}
		if j == 0 {
			return nil, "", false
		}
		id := s[:j]
		s = s[j:]

		allDigits := true
		for k := 0; k < len(id); k++ {
			if !isDigit(id[k]) {
				allDigits = false
				break
			}
		}
		var v semverID
		if allDigits {
			n, err := strconv.ParseUint(id, 10, 64)
			if err != nil {
				return nil, "", false
			}
			v = semverID{numeric: true, num: n}
		} else {
			v = semverID{str: id}
		}
		ids = append(ids, v)

		if len(s) > 0 && s[0] == '.' {
			s = s[1:]
			continue
		}
		break
	}
	return ids, s, true
}

// parseSemver parses a semantic version with an optional leading v/V prefix.
// It requires the MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH numeric segments, and accepts an optional
// prerelease and an optional build metadata section.
func parseSemver(s string) (semverVersion, bool) {
	if s == "" {
		return semverVersion{}, false
	}
	if s[0] == 'v' || s[0] == 'V' {
		s = s[1:]
		if s == "" {
			return semverVersion{}, false
		}
	}

	segments := make([]uint64, 0, 3)
	for len(segments) < 3 {
		j := 0
		for j < len(s) && isDigit(s[j]) {
			j++
		}
		if j == 0 {
			return semverVersion{}, false
		}
		n, err := strconv.ParseUint(s[:j], 10, 64)
		if err != nil {
			return semverVersion{}, false
		}
		segments = append(segments, n)
		s = s[j:]

		if len(s) > 0 && s[0] == '.' {
			if len(segments) >= 3 {
				return semverVersion{}, false
			}
			s = s[1:]
			continue
		}
		break
	}

END STEP
